# Research round 3 — asymmetric setups: trend-pullback continuation (S7), the slow breakout (S8) and the bar size after costs (walk-forward, SIMULATION ONLY)

Generated 2026-11-05 05:52 UTC by `scripts/run_research_round_3.py` (plan row R19; 18.7 min). Rules: Sprint 2c's runner (`scripts/run_research_v1c.py`'s own `run_series`) with the Trader Policy's Trader tier and brakes (plan row R18, the fleet cell `trader|brakes|none`; the Reference cell `vt10/30 crash15/90dh ladder` beside it for information) and, for the fixed rules, the pre-registered fixed-rule protocol variant (the exit bracket fixed, no train-trade qualification); everything else is Sprint 2c's (regime gate `hmm3 bear>=0.5 gate`, S0 filter S0 vol×3 spread p98 gap3 FOMC±6/6h, walk-forward train 540d → purge 7d → test 180d, fee 0.10% + slippage 0.050% per side). Data: PUBLIC OKX spot candles of nine markets (1H and 4H stored; 6H and 1D resampled from 1H) frozen at Sprint 2c's data end (bar close ≤ 2026-10-20T10:00:00+00:00). Holdout: the last 12 months (2025-10-20->2026-10-20), opened once and only for configurations that passed every gate at the Trader cell.

**Nothing here authorizes paper, demo or live trading.** A passing configuration is a hypothesis for the forward paper fleet, not a strategy to deploy. Every number is a simulation on historical public data.

## Executive summary (plain English)

- **What we did.** We tested the two asymmetric setups of the Trader Plan - H7: the trend-pullback continuation rule S7 with three variants; H8: the slow Turtle System 2 breakout S8 with Garner's channel variant - each written as a fixed rule (one parameter set, nothing to select) and judged under a pre-registered fixed-rule protocol variant (the 20-train-trade qualification that silenced round 2's slow rules is skipped; the exit bracket is fixed) at the Trader Policy's Trader budget with its brakes, under the plan's section 4.6 gates, on BTC-USDT and ETH-USDT first and the seven R12 markets as replication, on 1D and 6H bars; plus the bar-size comparison of the S1 and S2 families on 1D, 6H and 4H bars. Everything else is Sprint 2c's. Before anything was judged, the same unchanged code re-ran the configurations this round shares with Sprint 2c, the replication study and the Trader Policy study and matched their stored numbers in 49 of 49 cases. 216 configurations on 9 markets were judged on 2019-12-16->2026-10-20 after fees and slippage; the last 12 months (2025-10-20->2026-10-20) stayed untouched except for configurations that passed.
- **H7 - trend-pullback continuation (S7): failed walk-forward** on BTC-USDT and ETH-USDT (0 of 32 configurations passed every gate); on the seven replication markets: failed walk-forward (0 of 112 passed). Best on the primary markets: S7e BTC-USDT 1D [none] with Calmar 0.24, +2.0% a year at a 8.4% drawdown, missing Sharpe above 0.7, Calmar above 0.5, 75% of rolling 12-month windows positive. Beside hold + policy at the same budget its Calmar was higher in 5 of 144 configurations (median -0.11 against 0.34); it traded a median 10.9 times a year (the plan expected 10-25 on daily bars), with an average win 1.86 x the average loss. Directional reading over all 144 configurations: does not replicate in direction (5 of 144; the 5% level needs 83). Variants beside the base rule: S7e raised Calmar in 57 of 72, S7w raised Calmar in 50 of 72, S7t raised Calmar in 18 of 72; S7e, S7w replicate(s) in direction.
- **H8 - the slow breakout with wide stops (S8): failed walk-forward** on BTC-USDT and ETH-USDT (0 of 16 configurations passed every gate); on the seven replication markets: failed walk-forward (0 of 56 passed). Best on the primary markets: S8 BTC-USDT 6H [none] with Calmar 0.44, +3.9% a year at a 8.8% drawdown, missing Calmar above 0.5, at least 30 trades. Beside hold + policy at the same budget its Calmar was higher in 11 of 72 configurations (median 0.20 against 0.34); it traded a median 3.2 times a year (the plan expected 3-6 on daily bars), with an average win 2.56 x the average loss. Directional reading over all 72 configurations: does not replicate in direction (11 of 72; the 5% level needs 44). Variants beside the base rule: S8g raised Calmar in 31 of 72; none replicates in direction.
- **Holdout.** Nothing passed walk-forward, so the untouched 12 months were not opened for any new rule (the hold + policy controls' 12 months at the Trader budget are shown as the comparison line, as in every study).
- **Bar size.** The claim '6H is the cost-adjusted sweet spot' does not hold under the pre-registered reading: at the Trader budget 6H had the highest Calmar of the three bar sizes in 7 of 16 (rule, market, mode) comparisons at the 0.30 % gate cost and in 6 of 16 at the measured 0.20 % (at least 8 needed at both). In 8 of those comparisons one bar size produced no trade at all because the protocol's 20-train-trade qualification silenced every walk-forward window there (S2 BTC-USDT 1D [gate], S2 BTC-USDT 1D [none], S2 ETH-USDT 1D [gate], S2 ETH-USDT 1D [none], S2+S5 BTC-USDT 1D [gate], S2+S5 BTC-USDT 1D [none], S2+S5 ETH-USDT 1D [gate], S2+S5 ETH-USDT 1D [none]) - the same limit that stopped round 2's slow rules - so that bar size is untested, not beaten; setting it aside (a clarification applied after the run, the verdict unchanged) gives 7 of 16 and 7 of 16: the claim still does not hold. By family (untested bar sizes set aside; best bar size at 0.30 % / 0.20 %): S1 family (8 comparisons): 1D 3 / 2, 6H 5 / 3, 4H 0 / 3; S2 family (8 comparisons): 1D 0 / 0, 6H 2 / 4, 4H 6 / 4. Median Calmar by bar size at the Trader budget (0.30 % / 0.20 %): 1D 0.02 / 0.54 (6 trades a year, fees 0.2% a year); 6H 0.39 / 0.47 (20 trades a year, fees 1.3% a year); 4H 0.46 / 0.52 (24 trades a year, fees 1.6% a year). Two pre-registered-or-stated caveats: the tier's volatility window is 30 bars (30 days at 1D, 5 days at 4H), so the comparison also compares volatility estimators; and the 0.20 % column is the Trader Policy study's construction - the most-often-selected rule replayed over every window - which for a swept family also removes the per-window selection, so it is information beside the 0.30 % walk-forward, not the walk-forward at a lower cost.
- **Binding gates.** 75% of rolling 12-month windows positive (207 of 216), Calmar above 0.5 (192 of 216), Sharpe above 0.7 (191 of 216), profit factor above 1.2 (133 of 216). 216 judged configurations at Sprint 2c's own pass rate (4 of 108) would give about 8.0 passes by chance; the holdout and the forward fleet, not the pass count, carry the verdict.
- **Paper fleet.** Nothing fleet-eligible was confirmed, so the fleet's shortlist is unchanged. Nothing is approved for demo or live trading.

## Fixed before the first run (the pre-registration)

Pre-registration hash (text, file and configuration): `d94982fe2186de2121b479ef2644a40e33172fa88cb2a878db7e84345d84692d`; file `docs/research/research_round_3_preregistration.json` written 2026-11-05T05:14:14+00:00.

- **fixed_on**: 2026-11-05, before any run (plan row R19)
- **question**: Do the two asymmetric setups of the Trader Plan, section 5 - the trend-pullback continuation rule S7 and the slow breakout S8, each with its pre-registered variants, written as fixed rules and judged under the fixed-rule protocol variant at the Trader tier with the section 4.3 brakes - pass the section 4.6 gates on the Sprint 2c walk-forward of BTC-USDT and ETH-USDT, replicate on the seven R12 markets, and survive the untouched 12 months; and is 6H the cost-adjusted sweet spot for the S1 and S2 families among 1D, 6H and 4H bars?
- **why**: Plan section 2.6: dip buying has no stand-alone edge, and the only version the evidence supports is a pullback inside an uptrend entered when price turns back up with the stop under the dip (S7); the Donchian/Turtle family is the best-documented asymmetric design and the fewer-trades, wider-stop direction is the one that kept working in futures after 1990 and fits the fee budget (S8). Round 2 (R16) could not judge its slow rules because the protocol's selection step silences any window in which a variant traded fewer than 20 times in training (TF traded in 13 of 180 windows, S2+TF in 53); the owner's decision (docs/PLAN_STATUS.md, 'From R16', taken 2026-11-05) answers it both ways - a forward control account (R18) and this pre-registered fixed-rule protocol variant. The bar-size question: one unreviewed 2026 preprint finds 6H bars the cost-adjusted sweet spot for crypto trend rules while our own record is mixed (the S1 family and the R12 confirmations favoured 6H, the S2 family and the volume-confirmation sleeves sit on 4H).
- **protocol**: Sprint 2c's own runner (scripts/run_research_v1c.py loaded as a module, its run_series) with Sprint 2c's defaults for everything the cell and the fixed-rule protocol do not define - the S0 filter with the FOMC calendar, the regime-v2 entry gate (3-state HMM, bear >= 0.5, fitted on every window's train slice; modes none and gate), the walk-forward (train 540 d, purge 7 d, test 180 d), the 0.25 reduce-only rebalance, the +-30 % robustness replay of every grid parameter (the four S7 numbers, the three S8 numbers; the ATR window is a default, as for S1/S2) and the trade-order bootstrap. The FIXED-RULE PROTOCOL VARIANT for S7/S8 and their variants: every parameter is one pre-registered value (one combination), the exit bracket is fixed ('none': the rule's own exit and stop; 'trail3' for S7t) and never selected, and the 20-train-trade qualification is skipped (min_trades 0: every walk-forward window runs the rule's test slice - there is nothing to select, so no window is silenced). The runner's defaults are checked against the stored Sprint 2c run before the study runs; every job's protocol check is recorded with its results and the study stops on any mismatch the job does not explain (a tier is a recorded policy_sizing mismatch, the fixed-rule protocol recorded brackets / min_trades mismatches, 1D bars a recorded timeframes mismatch, never hidden).
- **tier**: the Trader Policy v1 Trader tier (plan row R18, section 4.1): a 20 % volatility target over 30 bars, half size in a crash state, at most 2 % of equity at risk on one stop (the cap reads the rule's own stop distance at the decision close), the entries-only Turtle ladder, with the section 4.3 brakes on (the hard stop, the entry caps, the loss limits, the heat cap) - the paper fleet's cell 'trader|brakes|none'. The Reference cell (today's vt10/30 crash15/90dh ladder, no brakes) runs beside it for information and for the reproduction of the stored numbers; it never opens a holdout for a new rule. - **brakes**: turtle x0.8@-10%, x0.64@-20%; hard stop -30%; entry caps 1/market/day, 3/day, none on the bar after an exit; loss limits 3%/24h, 6%/week, 10%/month; heat cap 6% (group 50%, corr >= 0.7)

Never a hypothesis: no holdout is opened for it and nothing from it joins the fleet; a 1D configuration that passes the gates is reported as 'passes walk-forward, holdout not opened' and is a candidate for a future pre-registered row

- **verdicts**: a configuration passes when it passes every section 4.6 gate at the Trader cell; a hypothesis is labelled on the primary markets - 'confirmed' when at least one of its configurations passes there and is confirmed by its holdout, 'passed walk-forward, contradicted by the holdout' when some pass and none is confirmed, 'failed walk-forward' when none passes - and the replication markets get the same label of their own; a confirmed passer anywhere joins the fleet (the R12 rule); the readings beside the label, never replacing it: (a) the directional reading against hold + policy on the same cell (Calmar higher in at least k of n judged configurations, k at the one-sided binomial 0.05 tail; indicative, the configurations share markets), on the primary and the replication markets separately; (b) per variant, the same sign test against its base rule over every series, mode and cell; (c) the R12 replication reading for every configuration that passed on a primary market; (d) trades a year beside the plan's expectation; (e) H8's pooled reading; (f) the Trader cell beside the Reference cell (exposure ratio, risk per trade, cap binding, gaps, re-entries); every hypothesis that does not end 'confirmed' gets a failure analysis computed from the numbers beside its comparison line
- **holdout**: the last 12 months (2025-10-20 -> 2026-10-20), opened once, only for a configuration that passes every section 4.6 gate at the Trader cell, only by the full pre-registered study (every hypothesis, market, timeframe, mode and cell, the bar-size comparison, with the reproduction, never --quick); the v1c verdict (positive return, PF > 1, >= 10 trades); the hold + policy control's 12 months at the Trader cell on every series are the comparison line (never judged, as in every study); every opening goes into an append-only ledger the report shows, each beside the earlier look at the same months by the stored hold control of that market (6H when the opening is at 1D, where no stored look at that bar size exists). CAVEAT, stated before the run: the holdout's falling direction is known for every market (hold + policy lost on all nine over those months), and both rules sit out downtrends by construction; a confirmation is necessary, not sufficient, and the paper fleet's forward record is the judge
- **fleet**: a confirmed passer at the Trader cell with at most 40 trades a year on a 4H or 6H series joins the paper fleet's shortlist as a NEW account with a source tag naming this study, the tier ('trader'), the brakes, 'no variant', the cell and the fixed-rule protocol variant, through the fleet loader's tier-tagged path (plan row R18b) extended to accept the fixed-rule protocol for registered fixed rules; the fleet must still load the amended shortlist, otherwise the file is restored and the report says so (the R12 rule); a 1D passer is RECORDED, not applied (the fleet runs 4H and 6H bars), as a decision for the owner; existing accounts are untouched; nothing joins demo or live
- **no_changes_after_results**: no gate, parameter, rule, market, threshold, cell or verdict rule changes after the results are in; if a gate looks wrong the report says so

### H7 — trend-pullback continuation (S7)

- **rule**: S7: uptrend = the close above its 100-day simple moving average and the 50-day average above the 100-day; pullback = a close below the 20-day simple moving average while the uptrend holds (it arms the rule and starts the pullback low; every later bar's low lowers it, the entry bar's included); entry = a close above the previous bar's high while the uptrend holds (armed on an earlier bar); stop = under the pullback low, floored at 2 x ATR(14) (the stop distance at the decision close is max(close - pullback low, 2 x ATR), fixed at entry as every rule's stop is); exit = a close below the 50-day average, or the stop; the uptrend failing while armed disarms; an exit bar may arm a new pullback at once. No other parameters. Every lookback is in calendar days converted to bars of the timeframe.
- **variants**: `S7e`: the pullback average is the 10-day exponential average instead of the 20-day simple one; `S7w`: the stop floor is 3 x ATR(14) instead of 2 x; `S7t`: the same rule under the protocol's trail3 exit bracket (a trailing stop that activates once the running high is +1 R above the entry and then follows it at 3 R), applied through the bracket parameter by the runner and the fleet alike; its exit is the trailing stop, the fast-average cross or the initial stop, whichever comes first
- **candidates**: ['S7', 'S7e', 'S7w', 'S7t']
- **markets**: ['BTC-USDT', 'ETH-USDT', 'XRP-USDT', 'DOGE-USDT', 'LINK-USDT', 'TRX-USDT', 'ADA-USDT', 'LTC-USDT', 'SOL-USDT']
- **primary_markets**: ['BTC-USDT', 'ETH-USDT']
- **replication_markets**: ['XRP-USDT', 'DOGE-USDT', 'LINK-USDT', 'TRX-USDT', 'ADA-USDT', 'LTC-USDT', 'SOL-USDT']
- **timeframes**: ['1D', '6H']
- **regime_modes**: ['none', 'gate']
- **comparison**: hold + policy sizing on the same market, timeframe and cell (the always-long control at the Trader tier with the brakes, run in this study; its one mode is 'none', so the [gate] configurations compare with the same control); each variant also beside the base S7 on the same series, mode and cell
- **expected_trades_per_year**: [10.0, 25.0]
- **why**: the only version of dip buying the evidence supports: a dip inside an uptrend, entered when price turns back up, stop under the dip

### H8 — the slow breakout with wide stops (S8)

- **rule**: S8: a slow Turtle System 2 breakout - entry on a close above the prior 55-day high (the channel excludes the current bar), exit on a close below the prior 20-day low, Garner's 5 x ATR(14) stop fixed at entry, no adds, no volume or volatility filter (unlike S2). No other parameters.
- **variants**: `S8g`: Garner's own channels: 90-day high entry, 45-day low exit
- **candidates**: ['S8', 'S8g']
- **markets**: ['BTC-USDT', 'ETH-USDT', 'XRP-USDT', 'DOGE-USDT', 'LINK-USDT', 'TRX-USDT', 'ADA-USDT', 'LTC-USDT', 'SOL-USDT']
- **primary_markets**: ['BTC-USDT', 'ETH-USDT']
- **replication_markets**: ['XRP-USDT', 'DOGE-USDT', 'LINK-USDT', 'TRX-USDT', 'ADA-USDT', 'LTC-USDT', 'SOL-USDT']
- **timeframes**: ['1D', '6H']
- **regime_modes**: ['none', 'gate']
- **comparison**: as H7: hold + policy sizing on the same market, timeframe and cell; the variant beside the base S8
- **expected_trades_per_year**: [3.0, 6.0]
- **trade_count_caveat**: stated before the run: at 2-3 entries a year (the trade-count check below) S8 cannot reach the gate of 30 trades over the out-of-sample years on one market, so the 'at least 30 trades' gate will bind on every S8 configuration; the gate stays as the plan fixed it and the report says where it binds; the pooled reading across the nine markets (pooled trades, pooled profit factor, pooled average win / average loss, median Calmar beside hold + policy) is reported as information, never as a verdict and never as a fleet ticket
- **why**: the fewer-trades, wider-stop direction kept working in futures after 1990 and fits the fee budget; it could not be judged by the round-2 protocol

Parked by the plan (not tested here), with the reason:

- **opening-range breakout**: slippage kills it (two independent replications lose the edge at two cents a share or spread plus slippage) and it needs a wide stock universe (plan section 2.6)
- **VCP (Minervini)**: no quantitative evidence; championship returns are top-of-distribution outcomes; a mechanical definition, if ever, is a later fleet test (plan section 2.6)
- **naive dip buying**: anti-trend: more than 60 % of 196 buy-the-dip variants had a lower Sharpe than holding over 60 years (AQR 2025), and continuation beats reversal in BTC at every horizon (plan section 2.6)
- **intraday rules**: no profitable intraday trend following in the BTC literature, and the fee budget (plan sections 2.6 and 2.9)

Protocol check of a Reference cell against the stored Sprint 2c run: every parameter the same (checked: brackets, fee, holdout_months, ladder_recovery_days, min_trades, no_trade_label, policy_sizing, positioning_source, purge_days, quick, rebalance_threshold, regime, regime_modes, select_by, slippage, strategies, test_days, timeframes, train_days). Every job's check is stored with its results: a tier is a recorded `policy_sizing` mismatch, the fixed-rule protocol recorded `brackets` / `min_trades` mismatches, 1D bars a recorded `timeframes` mismatch; the study stops on any mismatch a job does not explain.

Gates: Sprint 2c's (profit_factor_min 1.2; max_drawdown_max 0.2; sharpe_min 0.7; calmar_min 0.5; rolling_12m_positive_min 0.75; worst_calendar_year_min -0.1; min_trades 30; robust_share_min 0.5; min_year_days 60) with max drawdown ≤ 25 % and worst calendar year ≥ -15 % at the Trader tier (≤ 20 % and ≥ -10 % at the Reference cell), plus average win / average loss ≥ 2; expectancy in R reported, never gated.

**Failure analysis** (computed from the numbers above):

- Across its 144 judged configurations the rule family ended with a higher risk-adjusted result (Calmar) than hold + policy sizing at the same budget in 5 and a lower one in 139: the median Calmar was -0.11 against 0.34 for the control (-0.43), the median yearly return -1.2% against +5.0%, the median worst drawdown 12.0% against 14.0%, and the median share of positive rolling 12-month windows 35.7% against 61.9%; it beat the control by more than 0.1 Calmar (Sprint 2c's acceptance-clause yardstick) in 5 of 144.
- It traded a median 10.9 times a year (the plan expected 10-25 on daily bars), won a median 30% of its trades with a median average win of 1.86 x the average loss and a median expectancy of -0.07 R, and was in the market 17% of the time against 100% for the always-long control.
- By bar size: 1D: Calmar above the control in 5 of 72 (median Calmar -0.07, 6.6 trades a year); 6H: Calmar above the control in 0 of 72 (median Calmar -0.14, 18.4 trades a year).
- With the HMM regime gate on, Calmar was above the control in 2 of 72 configurations (without the gate: 3 of 72).
- By market: ADA-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; BTC-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; DOGE-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; ETH-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; LINK-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; LTC-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; SOL-USDT 5 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; TRX-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass; XRP-USDT 0 of 16 above the control, 0 pass.
- In the 2022 bear year the median result was -1.2% against -5.5% for the control.
- Gates missed most often: 75% of rolling 12-month windows positive (142 of 144), Sharpe above 0.7 (130 of 144), Calmar above 0.5 (130 of 144), profit factor above 1.2 (108 of 144).
- Reading: the setup is worse than simply holding the market with the same risk budget - its entries come after the move has started and its exits give back the trend's turns, so the time out of the market cost more than the drawdowns it avoided; the family is refuted on these markets under this protocol, not merely untested.

**Failure analysis** (computed from the numbers above):

- Across its 72 judged configurations the rule family ended with a higher risk-adjusted result (Calmar) than hold + policy sizing at the same budget in 11 and a lower one in 61: the median Calmar was 0.20 against 0.34 for the control (-0.21), the median yearly return +1.2% against +5.0%, the median worst drawdown 7.8% against 14.0%, and the median share of positive rolling 12-month windows 56.1% against 61.9%; it beat the control by more than 0.1 Calmar (Sprint 2c's acceptance-clause yardstick) in 3 of 72.
- It traded a median 3.2 times a year (the plan expected 3-6 on daily bars), won a median 38% of its trades with a median average win of 2.56 x the average loss and a median expectancy of 0.20 R, and was in the market 33% of the time against 100% for the always-long control.
- By bar size: 1D: Calmar above the control in 4 of 36 (median Calmar 0.21, 2.7 trades a year); 6H: Calmar above the control in 7 of 36 (median Calmar 0.17, 3.6 trades a year).
- With the HMM regime gate on, Calmar was above the control in 5 of 36 configurations (without the gate: 6 of 36).
- By market: ADA-USDT 0 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; BTC-USDT 2 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; DOGE-USDT 2 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; ETH-USDT 5 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; LINK-USDT 0 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; LTC-USDT 0 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; SOL-USDT 2 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; TRX-USDT 0 of 8 above the control, 0 pass; XRP-USDT 0 of 8 above the control, 0 pass.
- In the 2022 bear year the median result was -1.4% against -5.5% for the control.
- Gates missed most often: at least 30 trades (72 of 72), 75% of rolling 12-month windows positive (65 of 72), Calmar above 0.5 (62 of 72), Sharpe above 0.7 (61 of 72).
- Reading: the rule trades too rarely for the gates as fixed. It missed 'at least 30 trades' in 72 of 72 configurations because it fires a few times a year (median 3.2), so over the 4.3 out-of-sample years one market cannot produce 30 trades; the gate stayed as pre-registered and this is the place the report says it binds. Whether the rule has an edge is answered by the pooled reading and the comparison with hold + policy above, as information, never as a verdict: a rule this slow needs a forward control account or a pre-registered pooled protocol, which is an owner decision, not an edit of this round.

Pre-registered reading (taken literally): 6H had the highest Calmar in 7 of 16 comparisons at the gate cost and 6 of 16 at the measured cost (at least 8 needed at both): the claim **does not hold**. Best-bar-size counts (0.30 % / 0.20 %): 1D 3 / 3; 6H 7 / 6; 4H 6 / 7.

Clarification applied after the run (the verdict is the same either way): S2 BTC-USDT 1D [gate], S2 BTC-USDT 1D [none], S2 ETH-USDT 1D [gate], S2 ETH-USDT 1D [none], S2+S5 BTC-USDT 1D [gate], S2+S5 BTC-USDT 1D [none], S2+S5 ETH-USDT 1D [gate], S2+S5 ETH-USDT 1D [none] produced no trade because the protocol's 20-train-trade qualification silenced every walk-forward window at that bar size - the limit that stopped round 2's slow rules - so that bar size is untested there, not beaten, and its 0.20 % replay (the default parameters) is not shown. With those set aside: 6H best in 7 of 16 at the gate cost and 7 of 16 at the measured cost: the claim **still does not hold**. By family: S1 (8 comparisons, 0 with an untested bar size): 1D 3 / 2, 6H 5 / 3, 4H 0 / 3; S2 (8 comparisons, 8 with an untested bar size): 1D 0 / 0, 6H 2 / 4, 4H 6 / 4.

The 0.20 % column is the Trader Policy study's construction: the most-often-selected rule replayed over every window with the same gate and sizing. For a swept family that also removes the per-window selection, so it is information beside the 0.30 % walk-forward, not the walk-forward at a lower cost; a future bar-size study should pre-register a gate-cost replay of the same fixed rule beside it, and a fixed-rule protocol for a family the qualification silences at a bar size.

## Paper-fleet shortlist

Unchanged: no configuration was both a walk-forward passer at the Trader cell, confirmed by the untouched 12 months, and runnable by the fleet.

## Owner decisions (nothing below is decided by this report)

- **Fleet.** Nothing to add. Recommended: no change.
- **What the verdicts mean.** H7: failed walk-forward; H8: failed walk-forward on the primary markets; H7: failed walk-forward; H8: failed walk-forward on the replication markets. No gate, parameter, rule, market, cell or verdict rule changed after the results. If a gate looks wrong, that is a note for the next pre-registered round, not an edit of this one.
- **Slow rules and the 30-trade gate.** H8 missed 'at least 30 trades' in 72 of 72 configurations: a rule that fires a few times a year cannot produce 30 trades on one market over 4.3 out-of-sample years, whatever its edge. The gate stayed as pre-registered (the plan fixed it) and the pooled reading is information only. Whether to judge such a rule another way — a forward control account in the paper fleet, or a pre-registered pooled protocol over markets — is a decision for the owner (`docs/PLAN_STATUS.md`, section 2); nothing here is retuned.
- **Next round.** The failure analyses above say which setups are not worth another test; anything left credible is a decision for the owner, as a new plan row.

## Method notes and caveats

- The rules were not re-implemented and the runner was not changed: Sprint 2c's runner module ran them with its plan-row-R18 arguments; the reproduction table shows the same code gives the stored numbers of Sprint 2c, the replication study and the Trader Policy study where this round shares a configuration with them.
- The fixed-rule protocol variant applies only to S7, S8 and their variants: one parameter set each, the exit bracket fixed (`none`, or `trail3` for S7t) and never selected, and `min_trades 0` so that every walk-forward window runs the rule's test slice (there is nothing to select, so no window is silenced). The bar-size families keep Sprint 2c's selection, the 20-train-trade qualification included.
- Every lookback is in calendar days converted to bars of the timeframe; the +-30 % robustness replay perturbs every grid number (the four S7 numbers, the three S8 numbers), as it does for every rule.
- The tier's 30-bar volatility window is 30 days on 1D bars, 7.5 days on 6H and 5 days on 4H (Sprint 2b's definition, a protocol fact); the crash window and the ladder re-arm are in days. The S0 filter's one-day volatility leg is inert on 1D bars (a one-bar window).
- The regime gate's HMM is fitted on every window's train slice (deterministic), the FOMC calendar is the one checked in plan row R7, and the Trader tier and its brakes are exactly the Trader Policy study's; none of them was adapted to the new rules or to 1D bars.
- 216 judged configurations are 216 more comparisons on the same six years; at Sprint 2c's pass rate about 8.0 passes among them would be expected by chance, which is why the holdout and the forward fleet, not single passes, carry the verdict. The directional sign tests treat configurations as trials although they share markets, so they are indicative only.
- The holdout's falling direction was known for every market before this study; both rules sit out downtrends by construction. Confirmation there is necessary, not sufficient.
- Every number is a simulation on historical public data and proves nothing about the future.
- Honesty note: the first full run of this study (started 2026-11-05 05:14 UTC, right after the pre-registration) crashed before writing any result or ledger entry: the Sprint 2c runner's regime-threshold sensitivity replay sized the lead gated S1/S2 rule of a bar-size job with the policy spec but without the rule's stop, which a per-trade-cap tier needs (the Trader Policy study never ran plain S1/S2 at the Trader cell, so the path had never met a tier). The replay was made stop-aware in scripts/run_research_v1c.py (Sprint 2c's own policy reads no stop and takes exactly the path it always did; the reproduction table proves it) and the whole study was re-run with the pre-registration unchanged. Nothing of the first run was tabulated; its progress log was glanced at while it ran (two LINK-USDT 1D lines), no table and no verdict.
